. If a Coxeter label (mark)
d_{i}
is different from
d_{i}^{\vee}
, it is represented as a red number.

The 1-loop contribution to the prepotential of a 6d gauge theory on a circle is determined by matter representations of (twisted-)affine Lie algebras. Let us discuss the affine root systems. A root
\alpha
is called
real
if there exists an element
w
in the Weyl group such that
w(\alpha)
is a simple root. A root which is not real is called an
imaginary
root. It is known that there is no imaginary root for a simple Lie algebra
kac_infinite_2003
. To describe a root system of affine Lie algebras, let
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
be an affine Lie algebra of type
X_{\ell}^{(r)}
and
\hat{\Delta}
be its root system. We also define a simple Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}
obtained by removing the affine node
\alpha_{0}
in the Dynkin diagram of
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
and its root system
\Delta
. When
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=X_{\ell}^{(1)}
is untwisted,
\mathfrak{h}=\mathfrak{g}=X_{\ell}
, but if
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=X_{\ell}^{(r)}
is twisted, then
\mathfrak{h}
is different from
\mathfrak{g}=X_{\ell}
. The root system of
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
can be written in terms of the roots of
\mathfrak{h}

kac_infinite_2003
. First, define

\displaystyle\delta=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}d_{i}\alpha_{i}\ ,

(294)

where
d_{i}
are the Coxeter labels of
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
algebra. The set of imaginary roots of
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
is

\displaystyle\hat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{im}}=\{n\delta\mid n\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}\}\ .

(295)

The multiplicities of imaginary roots are as follows:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=X_{\ell}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\quad\text{multiplicity of }n\delta\text{ is }\ell

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=A_{2\ell}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\quad\text{multiplicity of }n\delta\text{ is }\ell

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=A_{2\ell-1}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\quad\text{multiplicity of }2n\delta\text{ is }\ell\text{, }(2n+1)\delta\text{ is }\ell-1

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=D_{\ell+1}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\quad\text{multiplicity of }2n\delta\text{ is }\ell\text{, }(2n+1)\delta\text{ is }1

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=E_{6}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\quad\text{multiplicity of }2n\delta\text{ is }4\text{, }(2n+1)\delta\text{ is }2

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=D_{4}^{(3)}

\displaystyle\quad\text{multiplicity of }3n\delta\text{ is }2\text{, }(3n\pm 1)\delta\text{ are }1\ .

(296)

On the other hand, sets of real roots are given as follows: if
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=X_{\ell}^{(1)}
, then

\displaystyle\hat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{re}}=\{\alpha+n\delta\mid\alpha\in\Delta,\ n\in\mathbb{Z}\}\,.

(297)

If
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=X_{\ell}^{(r=2,3)}
and
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}\neq A_{2\ell}^{(2)}
, then

\displaystyle\hat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{re}}=\{\alpha+n\delta\mid\alpha\in\Delta_{s},\ n\in\mathbb{Z}\}\cup\{\alpha+nr\delta\mid\alpha\in\Delta_{l},\ n\in\mathbb{Z}\}\ ,

(298)

where
\Delta_{l}
and
\Delta_{s}
denote the long and the short roots of
\mathfrak{h}
, respectively. If
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=A_{2\ell}^{(2)}
, then

\displaystyle\hat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{re}}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\frac{1}{2}\big(\alpha+(2n-1)\delta\big)\ \Big|\ \alpha\in\Delta_{l},\ n\in\mathbb{Z}\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\{\alpha+n\delta\mid\alpha\in\Delta_{s},\ n\in\mathbb{Z}\}\cup\{\alpha+2n\delta\mid\alpha\in\Delta_{l},\ n\in\mathbb{Z}\}\,.

(299)

The multiplicities of real roots are
1
. The total root system of an affine Lie algebra
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
is then given by
\hat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{re}}\cup\hat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{im}}
.

Using the affine root system we can compute the 1-loop contributions of 6d vector multiplets to the effective prepotential. We first identify
r\delta
as a KK-momentum for
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=X_{\ell}^{(r)}
. The reason for
r
factor is as follows: from the real root system (
297
)-(
A.1
), the total root system
\Delta
of the underlying simple Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}
is repeated for every
r\delta
. This reflects that
r\delta
is a KK-momentum.

For an untwisted affine algebra
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=X_{\ell}^{(1)}
, (
A.1
) and (
297
) implies that the 1-loop prepotential is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\text{1-loop}}=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta}\absolutevalue{\alpha\cdot\phi+ n \tau}^{3}\ ,

(300)

where
\Delta
is the root system of
X_{\ell}
algebra and
\phi
denotes gauge holonomies and
\tau
is the inverse of 6d circle radius. The
\ell
imaginary roots can be understood as Cartan elements in the adjoint representation of
X_{\ell}
algebra and their contribution to the prepotential are discarded because they only carry KK-charges and thus they provide only constant shifts to the prepotential.

Let us now discuss the twisted cases. For
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=X_{\ell}^{(2)}
and
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}\neq A_{2\ell}^{(2)}
, one can read from (
298
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\text{1-loop}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\quantity( \sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_s} \abs{\alpha\cdot\phi+ \frac{n \tau}{2}}^3 + \sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_l} \abs{\alpha\cdot\phi+ n \tau}^3 )

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\quantity( \sum_{\alpha\in\Delta} \abs{\alpha\cdot\phi+ n \tau}^3 + \sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_s} \abs{\alpha\cdot\phi+ \qty(n + \frac{1}{2})\tau}^3 )\ ,

(301)

where
\Delta
,
\Delta_{l}
and
\Delta_{s}
are sets of all the roots, the long roots and the short roots of
\mathfrak{h}
algebra respectively. The first term carrying integral KK-charges forms the root system of
\mathfrak{h}
algebra. The second term carrying half-integral KK-charges amounts to the rank-2 antisymmetric representation of
C_{\ell}
for
A^{(2)}_{2\ell-1}
and the fundamental representation of
B_{\ell}
and
F_{4}
for
D^{(2)}_{\ell+1}
and
E^{(2)}_{6}
, respectively. The imaginary roots correspond to Cartan elements in each representation. This situation can be interpreted as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rlrlrl}A_{2\ell-1}&\to C_{\ell}&\quad D_{\ell+1}&\to B_{\ell}&\qquad E_{6}&\to F_{4}\\
\mathbf{Adj}&\to\mathbf{Adj}_{0}\oplus\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}_{1/2}\,,&\mathbf{Adj}&\to\mathbf{Adj}_{0}\oplus\mathbf{F}_{1/2}\,,&\mathbf{Adj}&\to\mathbf{Adj}_{0}\oplus\mathbf{F}_{1/2}\ ,\end{array}

which are branching rules of the adjoint representation of
\mathfrak{g}
to
\mathfrak{h}
. Here, the subscript in a representation
{\bf r}
stands for KK-momentum shift. For instance, the vector multiplet contribution in the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisted compactification of
\mathfrak{su}(4)=\mathfrak{so}(6)
gauge theory is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\text{1-loop}}=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{e\in\mathbf{R}}\absolutevalue{n \tau+ e\cdot\phi}^{3}+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{w\in\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}}\absolutevalue{\qty(n+\frac{1}{2})\tau+ w\cdot\phi}^{3}\,,

(304)

where
\mathbf{R}
and
\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}
are the root system and the rank-2 anti-symmetric representation of
\mathfrak{sp}(2)
algebra, respectively. By evaluating the 1-loop contribution explicitly using the zeta function regularization, one can compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\text{1-loop}}=\frac{4}{3}\phi_{1}^{3}+2\phi_{1}^{2}\phi_{2}-3\phi_{1}\phi_{2}^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\phi_{2}^{3}-\frac{11}{24}\tau(2\phi_{1}^{2}-2\phi_{1}\phi_{2}+\phi_{2}^{2})\,,

(305)

up to constant terms.

For
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=D_{4}^{(3)}
, the l-loop contribution can be obtained in a similar fashion with
r=3
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\text{1-loop}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\quantity(\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_s} \abs{\alpha\cdot\phi+ \frac{n\tau}{3}}^3 + \sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_l} \abs{\alpha\cdot\phi+ n\tau}^3 )

(306)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\quantity[\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta} \abs{\alpha\cdot\phi+ n \tau}^3 + \sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_s} \qty( \abs{\alpha\cdot\phi\!+\! \qty(n \!+\! \tfrac{1}{3})\tau}^3 + \abs{\alpha\cdot\phi\!+\! \qty(n \!+\! \tfrac{2}{3})\tau}^3 ) ]\,.

The first term carrying integer KK-charge forms the root system of
\mathfrak{h}=G_{2}
algebra, while the second and third terms amount to the fundamental representation of
G_{2}
. This can be summarized as

\displaystyle D_{4}

\displaystyle\to G_{2}

\displaystyle\mathbf{Adj}

\displaystyle\to\mathbf{Adj}_{0}+\mathbf{F}_{1/3}+\mathbf{F}_{2/3}\,.

(307)

The prepotential in (
306
) after performing the zeta function regularization reduces to

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\text{1-loop}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{3}\phi_{1}^{3}+3\phi_{1}^{2}\phi_{2}-4\phi_{1}\phi_{2}^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\phi_{2}^{3}-\frac{5}{9}\tau(3\phi_{1}^{2}-3\phi_{1}\phi_{2}+\phi_{2}^{2})\,,

(308)

which is used in subsection
6.1
.

For
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}=A_{2\ell}^{(2)}
, one can read from the root system (
A.1
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\text{1-loop}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\quantity(\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_l} \abs{\frac{\alpha\cdot\phi}{2} + \frac{2n\!-\!1}{4}\tau}^3 + \sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_s} \abs{\alpha\cdot\phi+ \frac{n \tau}{2}}^3 + \sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_l} \abs{\alpha\cdot\phi+ n \tau}^3 )

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta}\absolutevalue{\alpha\cdot\phi+ n \tau}^{3}+\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_{l}}\absolutevalue{\frac{\alpha\cdot\phi}{2} + \qty(n+\frac{1}{4})\tau}^{3}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_{l}}\absolutevalue{\frac{\alpha\cdot\phi}{2} + \qty(n+\frac{3}{4})\tau}^{3}+\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_{s}}\absolutevalue{\alpha\cdot\phi+ \qty(n+\frac{1}{2})\tau}^{3}\right)\,.

(309)

The first term in the second line forms the root system of
C_{\ell}
algebra. The second and third terms carrying
\tau/4
and
3\tau/4
are half of the long roots of
C_{\ell}
, so they amount to the fundamental representation of
C_{\ell}
algebra. The last term carrying half-integral KK-charges amounts to the rank-2 antisymmetric representation. There is one more singlet carrying half-integral KK-momentum which arises from the imaginary root, so we can understand the situation as a branching of
A_{2\ell}
algebra into
C_{\ell}
algebra

\displaystyle A_{2\ell}

\displaystyle\to C_{\ell}

\displaystyle\mathbf{Adj}

\displaystyle\to\mathbf{Adj}_{0}\oplus\mathbf{F}_{1/4}\oplus\mathbf{F}_{3/4}\oplus\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}_{1/2}\oplus\mathbf{1}_{1/2}\,.

(310)

As an example, the vector multiplet contribution in
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist compactification of a
SU(3)
gauge field is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\text{1-loop}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{e\in\mathbf{R}}\absolutevalue{e\cdot\phi+n \tau}^{3}+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{w\in\mathbf{F}}\absolutevalue{w\cdot\phi+\qty(n + \frac{1}{4})\tau}^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{w\in\mathbf{F}}\absolutevalue{w\cdot\phi+\qty(n + \frac{3}{4})\tau}^{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{3}\phi_{1}^{3}-\frac{5}{16}\tau\phi_{1}^{2}\ .

(311)

This is used in subsection
3.2.3
.

A.2
Graded representations and 1-loop prepotential

To find a KK-momentum shift for a hypermultiplet, we first reinterpret the KK-momentum shifts for W-bosons we discussed in the previous subsection. Let
\mathfrak{g}=X_{\ell}
be a simple Lie algebra,
r=2,3
be an order of automorphism in a Dynkin diagram,
s=(s_{0},s_{1},\cdots,s_{\ell})
be non-negative relatively prime integers, and
m=r\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}d_{i}s_{i}
for the Coxeter labels
d_{i}
. For a given
s
, there is a unique outer automorphism
\sigma_{s,r}
of
\mathfrak{g}
up to conjugation such that
\sigma_{s,r}^{m}=1

kac_infinite_2003
.
r
is the smallest integer for which
\sigma_{s,r}^{r}
is an inner automorphism. An order
m
outer automorphism
\sigma_{s,r}
naturally induces the grading

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{m}}\mathfrak{g}_{j}\ ,

(312)

with
\commutator*{\mathfrak{g}_i}{\mathfrak{g}_j}\subset\mathfrak{g}_{i+j}
, where an element of
\mathfrak{g}_{j}
has an eigenvalue
e^{2\pi ij/m}
under
\sigma_{s,r}
. Thus, under twisted compactification of a 6d theory with gauge algebra
\mathfrak{g}
, the W-boson states in
\mathfrak{g}_{j}
acquire additional fractional KK-charge
j/m
. In particular, W-boson states in the invariant subalgebra
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
of
\sigma_{s,r}
have integral KK-charges. The affine root system demands
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
to be
\mathfrak{h}
defined in Appendix
A.1
. It is known that when
s=(1,0,0,\cdots,0)
, the invariant subalgebra
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
is isomorphic to
\mathfrak{h}
, and
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
-module
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
is isomorphic to an irreducible module with the highest weight
-\alpha_{0}

kac_infinite_2003
.

We now reproduce the branching rules with KK-momentum shifts of the adjoint representations in Appendix
A.1
, using the automorphism
\sigma_{s,r}
and grading (
312
). As a representative example, consider
\mathfrak{g}=A_{2\ell-1}
with
s=(1,0,\cdots,0)
,
r=2
. Under the corresponding outer automorphism
\sigma_{s,r}
,
\mathfrak{g}
is graded by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{g}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{1}\ ,

(313)

where
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
is
C_{\ell}
algebra and
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
corresponds to the rank-2 antisymmetric representation of
C_{\ell}
algebra. We can also explicitly construct
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
. Let
E_{\alpha}
be a ladder operator of
A_{2\ell-1}
algebra associated with a root
\alpha
. We write
E_{i}=E_{\alpha_{i}}
for a simple root
\alpha_{i}
. The Cartan generators
H_{i}
are given as
H_{i}=\alpha_{i}^{\vee}=\commutator*{E_{\alpha_i}}{E_{-\alpha_i}}
. Note that
\commutator{H_i}{E_j}=\left\langle{\alpha_{j}},{\alpha_{i}^{\vee}}\right\rangle E_{j}=a_{ij}E_{j}
, where
a_{ij}
is a Cartan matrix element. An automorphism
\mu
in a Dynkin diagram acts on the simple roots by
\mu(\alpha_{i})=\alpha_{2\ell-i}
, so we can use the outer automorphism
\sigma_{s,r}
as the induced map
\sigma_{s,r}(H_{i})=H_{2\ell-i}
and
\sigma_{s,r}(E_{i})=E_{2\ell-i}
by uniqueness. The invariant combinations of the Cartan generators under
\sigma_{s,r}
are

\displaystyle\tilde{H}_{i}=H_{i}+H_{2n-i}\ (1\leq i\leq\ell-1),\quad\tilde{H}_{\ell}=H_{\ell}\ ,

(314)

and they become the Cartan generators of
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
. In the case of the ladder operators corresponding to the simple roots, invariant and non-invariant combinations under
\sigma_{s,r}
are given as follows:

\displaystyle\{E_{i}+E_{2\ell-i},E_{\ell}\mid 1\leq i\leq\ell-1\}\subset\mathfrak{g}_{0}\,,\quad\{E_{i}-E_{2\ell-i}\mid 1\leq i\leq\ell-1\}\subset\mathfrak{g}_{1}\,.

(315)

The eigenvalues of such elements in
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
under the Cartan generators
\tilde{H}_{i}
amount to the simple root of
\mathfrak{g}_{0}=C_{\ell}
algebra, and hence the collection of charges makes the Cartan matrix of
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
, in this case
\mathfrak{sp(\ell)}
, as shown in Table
33
.

\mathfrak{g}_{0}

\mathfrak{g}_{1}

Charge

E_{1}+E_{2\ell}

E_{1}-E_{2\ell}

(2,-1,0,0,\cdots,0)

E_{2}+E_{2\ell-1}

E_{2}-E_{2\ell-1}

(-1,2,-1,0,\cdots,0)

\vdots

\vdots

\vdots

E_{\ell-1}+E_{\ell+1}

E_{\ell-1}-E_{\ell+1}

(0,\cdots,0,-1,2,-1)

E_{\ell}

(0,0,\cdots,0,-2,2)

Table 33
:
Classification of the ladder operators corresponding to the simple roots of
A_{2\ell-1}
under order 2 outer automorphism
\sigma
. The charge implies the eigenvalues under the Cartan generators
\tilde{H}_{i}
of
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
.

For a general root
\alpha
of
A_{2\ell-1}
,
\sigma_{s,r}(E_{\alpha})=u_{\alpha}E_{\mu(\alpha)}
, where
u_{\alpha}=\pm 1
. If
\alpha\neq\mu(\alpha)
, then

\displaystyle E_{\alpha}+u_{\alpha}E_{\mu(\alpha)}\in\mathfrak{g}_{0},\quad E_{\alpha}-u_{\alpha}E_{\mu(\alpha)}\in\mathfrak{g}_{1}\,.

(316)

When
\alpha=\mu(\alpha)
, then
E_{\alpha}\in\mathfrak{g}_{0}
and there is no corresponding element in
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
. By considering the eigenvalues of all such elements in
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
under
\tilde{H}_{i}
defined in (
314
), one can confirm that charges of the elements in
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
form the weight systems of the adjoint representation and the rank-2 antisymmetric representation, respectively. For
\mathfrak{g}=D_{\ell+1}
and
E_{6}
, one can treat them in a similar way as the
A_{2\ell-1}
case and obtain the corresponding branching rules.

As discussed for the affine root system in Appendix
A.1
, the
\mathfrak{g}=A_{2\ell}
case is rather involved. By setting
s=(1,0,\cdots,0)
to construct an outer automorphism that leaves
\mathfrak{h}=C_{\ell}
invariant, we get order 4 automorphism
\sigma_{s,r}
since the Coxeter label
d_{0}=2
. This map
\sigma_{s,r}
induces the grading

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{g}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{2}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{3}\ ,

(317)

where
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
is
C_{\ell}
algebra. To find
\mathfrak{g}_{1,2,3}
, we consider the following choice of
\sigma_{s,r}

Tachikawa:2011ch
:

\displaystyle\sigma_{s,r}(x)=-\Omega\,x^{T}\Omega^{-1}\,,

(318)

where
x
is a traceless
(2\ell+1)\times(2\ell+1)
matrix and

\displaystyle\Omega=\matrixquantity(\lx@physics@matrix 1 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & \mathbf{I}_{2\ell} \\ 0 & -\mathbf{I}_{2\ell} & 0\endlx@physics@matrix)\ ,

(319)

with a
2\ell\times 2\ell
identity matrix
\mathbf{I}_{2\ell}
. Explicitly, the action of
\sigma_{s,r}
on
x
in the matrix representation is as follows:

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c|ccccc}x_{11}&&\vec{y}_{1}&&\vec{y}_{2}&\\
\hline\cr\vec{z}_{1}{}^{T}&&A&&B&\\
\vec{z}_{2}{}^{T}&&C&&D&\end{array}\right)\overset{\sigma_{s,r}}{\longmapsto}\left(\begin{array}[]{c|ccccc}-x_{11}&&-\vec{z}_{2}&&\vec{z}_{1}&\\
\hline\cr-\vec{y}_{2}{}^{T}&&-D^{T}&&B^{T}&\\
\vec{y}_{1}{}^{T}&&C^{T}&&-A^{T}&\end{array}\right)\overset{\sigma_{s,r}}{\longmapsto}\left(\begin{array}[]{c|ccccc}x_{11}&&-\vec{y}_{1}&&-\vec{y}_{2}&\\
\hline\cr-\vec{z}_{1}{}^{T}&&A&&B&\\
-\vec{z}_{2}{}^{T}&&C&&D&\end{array}\right)

where
\vec{y}_{1}=(x_{12},\cdots,x_{1,\ell+1})
,
\vec{y}_{2}=(x_{1,\ell+2},\cdots,x_{1,2\ell+1})
,
\vec{z}_{1}=(x_{21},\cdots,x_{\ell+1,1})
,
\vec{z}_{2}=(x_{\ell+2,1},\cdots,x_{2\ell+1,1})
and
A,B,C,D
are
\ell\times\ell
matrices. The invariant subalgebra
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
lies in the
2\ell\times 2\ell
matrix (the lower right part of (
A.2
)) which consists of
A,B,C,D
, satisfying
D=-A^{T}
,
B=B^{T}
and
C=C^{T}
.
This naturally identifies with
C_{\ell}
algebra. Suitable linear combinations of
(\vec{y}_{1},\vec{y}_{2})
and
(\vec{z}_{1},\vec{z}_{2})
are in
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{3}
, and they form two fundamental representations of
C_{\ell}
. The remaining
\mathfrak{g}_{2}
is identified with the rank-2 anti-symmetric representation of
C_{\ell}
, because the lower right
2\ell\times 2\ell
matrix is nothing but an embedding of
A_{2\ell-1}
algebra and it is decomposed to the adjoint and rank-2 anti-symmetric representations of
C_{\ell}
algebra. As the adjoint representation belongs to
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
, this reproduces (
A.1
).

We extend the above argument to general representations. When a Lie algebra is graded as (
312
), the representation
\pi:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{gl}(V)
is said to be
compatible with the grading
, if the vector space
V
is decomposed as

\displaystyle V=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{m}}V_{j}\ ,

(329)

such that

\displaystyle\pi(x_{i})V_{j}\subset V_{i+j}\ ,

(330)

for each
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}_{m}
and any
x_{i}\in\mathfrak{g}_{i}

havlicek_representations_2009
. Consequently, for matter fields, the states in a representation space
V_{j}
acquire additional fractional KK-charge
j/m
, similar to the adjoint representation. As an example, consider a twisted compactification of
\mathfrak{g}=A_{2\ell-1}
type gauge theory coupled with matter in the (anti-)fundamental representation. Let
\pi:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbb{C}^{2\ell})
be the fundamental representation and
\{\ket{\mu_i}\mid 1\leq i\leq 2\ell\}
be the basis of the representation space
\mathbb{C}^{2\ell}
whose charges under the Cartan generators
\pi(H_{i})
form the weight system of the fundamental representation:

\displaystyle\pi(H_{i})\ket{\mu_1}=\delta_{i,1}\ket{\mu_1}\,,\quad

\displaystyle\pi(H_{i})\ket{\mu_j}=(-\delta_{i,j-1}+\delta_{i,j})\ket{\mu_j}\quad(1<j<2\ell)\,,

\displaystyle\pi(H_{i})\ket{\mu_{2\ell}}=-\delta_{i,2\ell-1}\ket{\mu_{2\ell}}\ .

(331)

We note that
\ket{\mu_{i+1}}=E_{-\alpha_{i}}\ket{\mu_i}
. An outer automorphism
\sigma_{s,r}
induced by Dynkin diagram automorphism acts on the Cartan generators as
\sigma_{s,r}(H_{i})=H_{2\ell-i}
, so the charges of basis vectors
\ket{\mu_i}
under the Cartan generators
\pi^{*}(H_{i}):=\pi\circ\sigma_{s,r}(H_{i})=\pi(\sigma_{s,r}(H_{i}))
form the weight system of antifundamental representation:

\displaystyle\pi^{*}(H_{i})\ket{\mu_1}=\delta_{i,2\ell-1}\ket{\mu_1},

\displaystyle\pi^{*}(H_{i})\ket{\mu_j}=(\delta_{i,2\ell-j}-\delta_{i,2\ell+1-j})\ket{\mu_j}\ (1<j<2\ell)\ ,

\displaystyle\pi^{*}(H_{i})\ket{\mu_{2\ell}}=-\delta_{i,1}\ket{\mu_{2\ell}}.

(332)

Hence, the representation
\pi^{*}
is naturally identified to the anti-fundamental representation. The representation
\pi\oplus\pi^{*}
which is of the representation space
\mathbb{C}^{4\ell}
is then compatible with the grading (
313
). To see it, let
\ket{\mu_i}
be an embedding of the basis of
\pi
representation space
\mathbb{C}^{2\ell}
into
\mathbb{C}^{4\ell}
satisfying (
A.2
). Similarly, let
\ket{\nu_i}
be an embedding of the basis of
\pi^{*}
representation space
\mathbb{C}^{2\ell}
into
\mathbb{C}^{4\ell}
satisfying (
A.2
). Then we find that charges of
\ket{\mu_i}\pm\ket{\nu_i}
under the Cartan generators
\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(\tilde{H}_{i})
of the invariant subalgebra
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
are given as

\displaystyle(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(\tilde{H}_{i}))(\ket{\mu_1}\pm\ket{\nu_1})

\displaystyle=\delta_{i,1}(\ket{\mu_1}\pm\ket{\nu_1})\,,

\displaystyle(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(\tilde{H}_{i}))(\ket{\mu_j}\pm\ket{\nu_j})

\displaystyle=(-\delta_{i,j-1}+\delta_{i,j})(\ket{\mu_j}\pm\ket{\nu_j})\quad(1<j\leq\ell)\,,

\displaystyle(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(\tilde{H}_{i}))(\ket{\mu_j}\pm\ket{\nu_j})

\displaystyle=(\delta_{i,2\ell-j}-\delta_{i,2\ell+1-j})(\ket{\mu_j}\pm\ket{\nu_j})\quad(\ell<j<2\ell)\,,

\displaystyle(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(\tilde{H}_{i}))(\ket{\mu_{2\ell}}\pm\ket{\nu_{2\ell}})

\displaystyle=-\delta_{i,1}(\ket{\mu_{2\ell}}\pm\ket{\nu_{2\ell}})\,.

(333)

Here, we see that the representation space
\mathbb{C}^{4\ell}
of
\pi\oplus\pi^{*}
is decomposed into
\mathbb{C}^{2\ell}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{2\ell}
spanned by
\{\ket{\mu_i}+\ket{\nu_i}\}
and
\{\ket{\mu_i}-\ket{\nu_i}\}
, respectively.
Therefore, the eigenvalues of the basis vectors form the weight system of the fundamental representation of the invariant subalgebra
\mathfrak{g}_{0}=C_{\ell}
. Moreover, if one applies element
\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(E_{-\alpha_{i}}-E_{-\alpha_{2\ell-i}})
in
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
to
\ket{\mu_i}\pm\ket{\nu_i}
, then

\displaystyle(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(E_{-\alpha_{i}}-E_{-\alpha_{2\ell-i}}))(\ket{\mu_i}\pm\ket{\nu_i})=\ket{\mu_{i+1}}\mp\ket{\nu_{i+1}}\quad(1\leq i<\ell)\,.

(334)

Thus, the representation
\pi\oplus\pi^{*}
is compatible with the grading. If we identify one fundamental representation of
C_{\ell}
to integer KK-momentum modes, then the other one corresponds to half-integer KK-momentum modes,

\displaystyle A_{2\ell-1}

\displaystyle\to C_{\ell}

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}\oplus\overline{\mathbf{F}}

\displaystyle\to\mathbf{F}_{0}\oplus\mathbf{F}_{1/2}\,.

(335)

Next, consider the twisted compactification of
\mathfrak{g}=A_{2\ell}
type gauge algebra coupled with (anti-)fundamental matter. To use the explicit definition of
\sigma_{s,r}
(
318
), we note that a matrix representation of
H_{i}
of
\mathfrak{g}
is given by

\displaystyle(H_{i})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=\delta_{i\alpha}\delta_{i\beta}-\delta_{i+1,\alpha}\delta_{i+1,\beta}\ ,

(336)

where
1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq 2\ell+1
. The automorphism
\sigma_{s,r}
acts on them by

\displaystyle\sigma_{s,r}(H_{1})=-\sum_{i=1}^{\ell+1}H_{i}\,,\quad\sigma_{s,r}(H_{j})=-H_{\ell+j}\ (2\leq j\leq\ell)\,,

\displaystyle\sigma_{s,r}(H_{\ell+1})=\sum_{i=2}^{2\ell}H_{i}\,,\quad\sigma_{s,r}(H_{j})=-H_{j-\ell}\ (\ell+2\leq j\leq 2\ell)\,.

(337)

The Cartan generators of the invariant subalgebra
\mathfrak{g}_{0}=C_{\ell}
are

\displaystyle\tilde{H}_{i}=H_{i+1}-H_{\ell+i+1}\ (1\leq i\leq\ell-1)\,,\quad\tilde{H}_{\ell}=\sum_{i=\ell+1}^{2\ell}H_{i}\,.

(338)

Now, let
\pi:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbb{C}^{2\ell+1})
,
\pi(x)=x
be the fundamental representation and
\{\ket{\mu_i}\mid 1\leq i\leq 2\ell+1\}
be a basis of
\mathbb{C}^{2\ell+1}
whose eigenvalues form the weight system of the representation

\displaystyle\pi(H_{i})\ket{\mu_1}=\delta_{i,1}\ket{\mu_1}\,,

\displaystyle\pi(H_{i})\ket{\mu_j}=(-\delta_{i,j-1}+\delta_{i,j})\ket{\mu_j}\quad(1<j<2\ell+1)\,,

\displaystyle\pi(H_{i})\ket{\mu_{2\ell+1}}=-\delta_{i,2\ell}\ket{\mu_{2\ell+1}}\,.

(339)

Explicitly,
(\ket{\mu_i})_{\alpha}=\delta_{i\alpha}
for
1\leq\alpha\leq 2\ell+1
. The composition
\pi\circ\sigma_{s,r}
is the anti-fundamental representation:

\displaystyle\pi(\sigma(H_{i}))\ket{\mu_1}=-\delta_{i,1}\ket{\mu_1}\,,

\displaystyle\pi(\sigma(H_{i}))\ket{\mu_j}=(\delta_{i,\ell+j-1}-\delta_{i,\ell+j})\ket{\mu_j}\quad(2\leq j\leq\ell)\,,

\displaystyle\pi(\sigma(H_{i}))\ket{\mu_{\ell+1}}=\delta_{i,2\ell}\ket{\mu_{\ell+1}}\,,

\displaystyle\pi(\sigma(H_{i}))\ket{\mu_{j}}=(\delta_{i,j-\ell-1}-\delta_{i,j-\ell})\ket{\mu_{j}}\quad(\ell+2\leq j\leq 2\ell)\,.

(340)

Similar to the
A_{2\ell-1}
case, the direct sum representation
\pi\oplus\pi^{*}
is compatible with the grading (
317
). To see it, let
\ket{\mu_i}
be an embedding of the basis of the fundamental representation space
\mathbb{C}^{2\ell+1}
into the direct sum representation space
\mathbb{C}^{4\ell+2}
satisfying (
A.2
). Likewise, let
\ket{\nu_i}
be an embedding of the basis of the anti-fundamental representation space into
\mathbb{C}^{4\ell+2}
satisfying (
A.2
). Following
havlicek_representations_2009
, define a
(4\ell+2)\times(4\ell+2)
matrix

\displaystyle R=\matrixquantity(\lx@physics@matrix 0 & \mathbf{I}_{2\ell+1} \\ \Omega^2 & 0\endlx@physics@matrix)\ ,

(341)

which satisfies

\displaystyle(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(\sigma(x)))=R(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(x))R^{-1}\ ,

(342)

where

\displaystyle\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(x)=\matrixquantity(\lx@physics@matrix x & 0 \\ 0 & -\Omega x^T \Omega^{-1}\endlx@physics@matrix)\in\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbb{C}^{4\ell+2})\,.

(343)

Since
R^{4}
is an identity matrix, it decomposes
\mathbb{C}^{4\ell+2}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}^{4\ell+2}=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{4}}V_{j}\ ,

(344)

where an element of
V_{j}
has an eigenvalue
e^{2\pi i(n+j)/4}
for some fixed integer
n
under
R
. Moreover, for
x_{j}\in\mathfrak{g}_{j}
and
v_{k}\in V_{k}
,

\displaystyle\Big(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}\big(\sigma(x_{j})\big)\Big)Rv_{k}=e^{2\pi i(n+j+k)/4}\big(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(x_{j})\big)v_{k}=R\big(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(x_{j})\big)v_{k}\ ,

(345)

from (
342
). This shows that
(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(x_{j}))v_{k}\in V_{j+k}
, so the grading on representation space is compatible with Lie algebra grading. The remaining thing is to identify each
V_{j}
into a representation of the invariant subalgebra. The states
\ket{\mu_1}\pm\ket{\nu_1}
have eigenvalues
\pm 1
under
R
, and
\ket{\mu_j}\pm i\ket{\nu_j}
have eigenvalues
\pm i
for
j>1
. The charges of these states under the Cartan generators of the invariant subalgebra are

\displaystyle(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(\tilde{H}_{k}))(\ket{\mu_1}\pm\ket{\nu_1})=0\,,

(346)

\displaystyle(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(\tilde{H}_{k}))(\ket{\mu_2}\pm i\ket{\nu_2})=\delta_{k,1}(\ket{\mu_2}\pm i\ket{\nu_2})\,,

\displaystyle(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(\tilde{H}_{k}))(\ket{\mu_j}\pm i\ket{\nu_j})=(-\delta_{k,j-2}+\delta_{k,j-1})(\ket{\mu_j}\pm i\ket{\nu_j})\ (2\leq j\leq\ell+1)\,,

\displaystyle(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(\tilde{H}_{k}))(\ket{\mu_{\ell+2}}\pm i\ket{\nu_{\ell+2}})=-\delta_{k,1}(\ket{\mu_{\ell+2}}\pm i\ket{\nu_{\ell+2}})\,,

\displaystyle(\pi\oplus\pi^{*}(\tilde{H}_{k}))(\ket{\mu_j}\pm i\ket{\mu_j})=(\delta_{k,j-\ell-2}-\delta_{k,j-\ell-1})(\ket{\mu_j}\pm i\ket{\nu_j})\ (\ell+3\leq j\leq 2\ell+1)\,.

Thus, the representation space spanned by
\{\ket{\mu_1}+\ket{\nu_1}\}
and
\{\ket{\mu_1}-\ket{\nu_1}\}
correspond to two singlets, while
\{\ket{\mu_j}+i\ket{\nu_j}\}
and
\{\ket{\mu_j}-i\ket{\nu_j}\}
correspond to two fundamental representations of
C_{\ell}
. We choose
n=-1
in the eigenvalue
e^{2\pi i(n+j)/4}
of the elements
V_{j}
in (
344
) so that
V_{0}
and
V_{2}
correspond to fundamental matter, while
V_{1}
and
V_{3}
become singlets. This is because the 5d reduction of the theory contains fundamental matter. In other words, under twisted compactification,

\displaystyle A_{2\ell}

\displaystyle\to C_{\ell}

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}\oplus\overline{\mathbf{F}}

\displaystyle\to\mathbf{F}_{0}\oplus\mathbf{1}_{1/4}\oplus\mathbf{F}_{1/2}\oplus\mathbf{1}_{3/4}\,.

(347)

We summarize such rules for twisted compactifications in Table
34
.

:

,

and

Appendix B
5-brane webs for theories of frozen singularities

In this section, we present 5-brane webs for some theories of frozen singularities, which contain an
O7^{+}
-plane.

B.1
5-brane webs for
SU(2)_{\pi}+1\mathbf{Adj}
and local
\mathbb{P}^{2}+``1\mathbf{Adj}"

A 5-brane configuration for
SU(2)_{\pi}+1\mathbf{Adj}
is depicted in Figure
9
(a). A little bit of deformation of this 5-brane web leads to a 5-brane web given in Figure
9
(b). By taking the
(1,-1)
7-brane painted in red through the cut of an
O7^{+}
-plane, one gets a 5-brane web in Figure
9
(c). Notice here that on the left hand side, the 5-brane configuration looks like
{\rm d}\mathbb{P}_{1}
geometry locally. Hence, if one decouples the adjoint hypermultiplet by taking an
O7^{+}
far away, then one obtains a 5-brane web for the pure
SU(2)_{\pi}
theory as expected. On the other hand, recalling that
{\rm d}\mathbb{P}_{1}
has an
\mathcal{O}(-1)
curve, we can flop this
-1
curve, which gives a 5-brane web given in Figure
9
(d). By taking this flopped part away, one finds that the remaining part is a local
\mathbb{P}^{2}
with the adjoint hypermultiplet inherited from
SU(2)_{\pi}+1\mathbf{Adj}
, as depicted in Figure
9
(e). which we have referred to as
\mathbb{P}^{2}+``1\mathbf{Adj}"
. One can also view this decoupling as decoupling the ‘instantonic’ hypermultiplet as discussed in the main text.

Figure 9
:
(a) A 5-brane web for
SU(2)_{\pi}+1\mathbf{Adj}
. (b) An equivalent 5-brane web for
SU(2)_{\pi}+1\mathbf{Adj}
where the D5 brane located on the right hand side of an
O7^{+}
-plane in figure (a) is moved to the left hand side. (c) Hanany-Witten transition associated with the (1,1) 7-brane painted in blue in figure (b), going through the cut of the
O7^{+}
-plane. (d) A flop transition. (e) Decoupling the flopped ‘instantonic’ hypermultiplet, giving rise to a 5-brane web for the local
\mathbb{P}^{2}+``1\mathbf{Adj}"
theory. Clearly putting an
O7^{+}
-plane far away leads to a local
\mathbb{P}^{2}
which corresponds to decoupling “
1\mathbf{Adj}
".

B.2
5-brane web for
SU(2)_{0}+1\mathbf{Adj}

SU(2)_{0}+1\mathbf{Adj}
is a KK theory which is also referred to as the M-string. The corresponding web, depicted in Figure
10
(c), was discussed in
Haghighat:2013gba
. We note that one also depicts it with an
O7^{+}
-plane as given in Figure
10
(a). It is not difficult to see that the Kähler parameters of these two 5-brane webs are equivalent as shown in Figure
10
(b) and Figure
10
(c).

Figure 10
:
(a) A 5-brane web for
SU(2)_{0}+1\mathbf{Adj}
. (b) The reflected image of (a) is included. (c) A 5-brane configuration for the M-string.

B.3
5-brane web for
Sp(2)_{0}+1\mathbf{Adj}

A 5-brane web for 5d
Sp(2)_{0}+1\mathbf{Adj}
can be obtained by a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisting of the 6d
\mathcal{N}=(2,0)

A_{3}
theory whose brane configuration can be realized as a
D6
brane suspended between 4
NS5
branes, as depicted in Figure
11
(a).

Figure 11
:
(a) Type IIA configuration for 6d
SU(1)-SU(1)-SU(1)
theory or equivalently
SU(4)
theory. (b) A
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisted compactification of (a), where 1/2
D5
-branes are stuck along the cut of an
O7^{+}
-plane. (c) Type IIB 5-brane configuration after resolving an
O7^{-}
into two 7-branes of charge
(1,1)
and
(1,-1)
. Here a blue dot refers to a
(1,1)
7-brane while a red dot refers to a
(1,-1)
7-brane.

The procedure of a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisting of the 6d
\mathcal{N}=(2,0)

A_{N}
theory is already considered in
Hayashi:2015vhy
. Such twisted compactification on 5-brane gives rise to a pair of
O7^{-}
- and
O7^{+}
-planes and the number of
D6
branes in Type IIA brane configuration is halved to yield half the number of
D5
-branes. For this
A_{3}
case, the resulting 5-brane configuration is given in Figure
11
(b), where a half
D5
is stuck along the cut of an
O7^{+}
-plane. By resolving an
O7^{-}
into two 7-branes of charges
(1,1)
and
(1,-1)
, one gets a 5-brane configuration for
Sp(2)_{0}+1\mathbf{Adj}
as depicted in Figure
11
(c). We note that in Figure
11
(c), we moved two half
D5
-branes on the right hand side of an
O7^{+}
to the left to form a full
D5
-brane so that they then can be away from the
O7^{+}
cut at the bottom, which allows us to give the mass of the adjoint hypermultiplet.

B.4
5-brane web for
Sp(2)_{\pi}+1\mathbf{Adj},~SU(3)_{\frac{3}{2}}+1\mathbf{Sym}

A 5-brane web for
Sp(2)_{\pi}+1\mathbf{Adj}
can be obtained a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisted compactification of the 6d
\mathcal{N}=(2,0)

A_{4}
theory. The resulting web is given in Figure
12
, where two half
D5
branes are suspended either between an
O7^{+}
and
(1,1)
5-brane or between an
O7^{+}
and
(-2,1)
5-brane.

Figure 12
:
A 5-brane web for
Sp(2)_{\pi}+1\mathbf{Adj}
or
SU(3)_{\frac{3}{2}}+1\mathbf{Sym}
.

As before, two half
D5
-branes can be put together to form a full
D5
-brane. It can be then easily recognized that the resulting 5-brane configuration is nothing but that for
SU(3)_{\frac{3}{2}}+1\mathbf{Sym}
as given in Figure
13
(a).

Figure 13
:
(a) A 5-brane web for
SU(3)_{\frac{3}{2}}+1\mathbf{Sym}
which has a
-1
curve on the left hand side of an
O7^{+}
-plane. (b) A flop transition and allocating a D5-brane from the right to the left. (c) Decoupling the ‘instantonic’ hypermultiplet, which leads to a 5-brane configuration for a non-Lagrangian theory,
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{3}+``1\mathbf{Sym}."

B.5
5-brane web for
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{3}+``1\mathbf{Sym}"
or
``SU(3)_{\frac{3}{2}}+1\mathbf{Sym}-1\mathbf{F}"

In a similar way as done for the 5-brane configuration for
\mathbb{P}^{2}+1\mathbf{Adj}
, one can easily get a 5-brane configuration for
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{3}+``1\mathbf{Sym}"
or
``SU(3)_{\frac{3}{2}}+1\mathbf{Sym}-1\mathbf{F}"
as depicted in Figure
13
. Given a 5-brane configuration for
SU(3)_{\frac{3}{2}}+1\mathbf{Sym}
in Figure
13
(a), which has a
-1
curve, one performs a flop transition to yield 5-brane web in Figure
13
(b). Decoupling this ‘instantonic’ hypermultiplet gives rise to
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{3}+``1\mathbf{Sym}"
whose 5-brane web is given in Figure
13
(c), which we may be referred to as
``SU(3)_{\frac{3}{2}}+1\mathbf{Sym}-1\mathbf{F}."

B.6
5-brane web for
SU(3)_{0}+1\mathbf{Sym}+1\mathbf{F}

A 5-brane configuration for
SU(3)_{0}+1\mathbf{Sym}+1\mathbf{F}
is depicted in Figure
14
, which is discussed in detail in
Hayashi:2018lyv
.

Figure 14
:
A 5-brane configuration for
SU(3)_{0}+1\mathbf{Sym}+1\mathbf{F}
.

B.7
5-brane web for local
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}+``1\mathbf{Sym}"

A 5-brane web for
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}+``1\mathbf{Sym}"
is depicted in Figure
16
(e). It is worthy of noting that this 5-brane web can be obtained from a non-perturbative Higgsing of
SU(4)_{0}+1\mathbf{Sym}
in Figure
15
.

Figure 15
:
(a) A 5-brane web for
SU(4)_{0}+1\mathbf{Sym}
. (b) A non-perturbative Higgsing by putting two
NS5
-branes together and perform a Higgsing such that the
NS5
-brane ending two
(0,1)
7-branes is taken away along the
x^{7,8,9}
-directions.

By taking a non-perturbative Higgsing
11
11

11

A simple non-perturbative Higgsing procedure is from
SU(3)_{0}+5\mathbf{F}
to
SU(2)+5\mathbf{F}
where two parallel
NS5
-branes are bound together in such a way that the resulting 5-brane web preserves the S-rule
Hayashi:2013qwa
;
Kim:2014nqa
.
shown in Figure
15
(b), we get a new rank-2 non-Lagrangian theory,
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}+``1\mathbf{Sym}"
. To see local geometry apart from the frozen singularity, consider a series of deformations of the 5-brane web given in Figure
16
.
Lowering the
D5
-brane on the right side of an
O7^{+}
-plane leads to a transition like Figure
16
(a) discussed in
Hayashi:2017btw
giving rise to a 5-brane web in Figure
16
(b). A further lowering of the
D5
-brane allocates the
D5
-brane from the right to the bottom left as depicted in Figure
16
(c). The resulting 5-brane configuration then looks like locally
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}
. Taking into account
1\mathbf{Sym}
inherited from
SU(4)_{0}+1\mathbf{Sym}
, we call this
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}+``1\mathbf{Sym}"
.

Figure 16
:
Deformations of the 5-brane web given in Figure
15
(b) leading to a 5-brane web for
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}+``1\mathbf{Sym}"
.

We note that it is straightforward to implement this kind of non-perturbative Higgsing to
SU(N)_{\frac{4-N}{2}}+1\mathbf{Sym}
to yield rank
N-1
non-Lagrangian theory with a frozen singularity,
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}\cup\mathbb{F}_{8}\cup\cdots\cup\mathbb{F}_{2N}+``1\mathbf{Sym}"
, as shown in Figure
17
.

Figure 17
:
(a) A 5-brane web for
SU(N)_{\frac{4-N}{2}}+1\mathbf{Sym}
, where there are
N
D5-branes in total. (b) A non-perturbative Higgsing. (c) 5-brane web for
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}\cup\mathbb{F}_{8}\cup\cdots\cup\mathbb{F}_{2N}+``1\mathbf{Sym}"
which is rank of
N-2
.

Figure 18
:
Various phases of 5-brane web for local
\mathbb{P}^{2}+``1\mathbf{Adj}"
.

In particular, when
N=2
, it provides yet another way of obtaining a 5-brane web for the local
\mathbb{P}^{2}+``1\mathbf{Adj}"
theory giving rise to various phases for the theory as depicted in Figure
18
(a)-(d). Note that a 5-brane configuration in Figure
18
(a) looks as if all W-bosons are legitimate so that the resulting theory is a Lagrangian theory, but it appears that some of the W-bosons would be annihilated so that the corresponding system is that of non-Lagrangian theory.

Figure 19
:
From 5-brane web for local
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}+``1\mathbf{Sym}+2\mathbf{F}"
to that for
SU(3)_{0}+1\mathbf{Sym}+1\mathbf{F}
. (a) A 5-brane web for local
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}+``1\mathbf{Sym}+2\mathbf{F}"
. (b) Locating D5-brane on the right-hand side of
O7^{+}
to the left. (c) Pull down the
(0,1)
7-brane (in red) in figure (b) toward the cut of
O7^{+}
and perform an
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
transformation. (d) With some mass deformations, Hanany-Witten move with the
(-3,1)
7-brane in figure (c) which gives rise to an 5-brane web for
SU(3)_{0}+1\mathbf{Sym}+1\mathbf{F}
. (d) A little deformation leading to the same web for
SU(3)_{0}+1\mathbf{Sym}+1\mathbf{F}
given in Figure
14
.

Equivalence between
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}+``1\mathbf{Sym}+2\mathbf{F}"
and
SU(3)_{0}+1\mathbf{Sym}+1\mathbf{F}

We can show that
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}+``1\mathbf{Sym}+2\mathbf{F}"
and
SU(3)_{0}+1\mathbf{Sym}+1\mathbf{F}
are equivalent by transforming a 5-brane web for
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}+``1\mathbf{Sym}+2\mathbf{F}"
into that of
SU(3)_{0}+1\mathbf{Sym}+1\mathbf{F}
that is given in Figure
14
. Consider a 5-brane web for
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}+``1\mathbf{Sym}+2\mathbf{F}"
which is to add two flavor
D7
-branes to
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}+``1\mathbf{Sym}"
in Figure
15
(b). This resulting 5-brane web is depicted in Figure
19
(a). The deformations given in Figure
19
(b) to
19
(d) lead to a 5-brane web for
SU(3)_{0}+1\mathbf{Adj}+1\mathbf{F}
in Figure
19
(e) which is the same as the one in Figure
14
. This clearly suggests that there is a new RG flow from
SU(3)_{0}+1\mathbf{Sym}+1\mathbf{F}
to local
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}+``1\mathbf{Sym}+1\mathbf{F}"
.

The above equivalence relation is readily generalized to the following
SU(N)_{0}
KK theory,

\displaystyle SU(N)_{0}+1\mathbf{Sym}+(N-2)\mathbf{F}

(348)

is equivalent to

(349)

\displaystyle\text{local }\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{6}\cup\mathbb{F}_{8}\cup\cdots\cup\mathbb{F}_{2N-2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{2N}+``1\mathbf{Sym}+(N-1)\mathbf{F}".

(350)

We note that when
N=2
, the equivalence reads

\displaystyle SU(2)_{\pi}+1\mathbf{Adj}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\text{local }\mathbb{P}^{2}+1\mathbf{Adj}+1\mathbf{F}.

(351)
